> AGENT

Yeah, that's pretty wild — 171 changelog entries across 4 packages. This is because release-please was just set up (phase 41) and there are no prior release tags, so it's treating the **entire commit history** as unreleased changes for each package.

The fix is to "bootstrap" release-please by giving it a starting point. You have two options:

**Option 1: Set `last-release-sha` in the config** — tells release-please to only consider commits after a specific SHA:

```json
// in release-please-config.json, per package:
"apps/api": {
  ...
  "last-release-sha": "<sha-of-commit-before-phase-41>"
}
```

**Option 2: Create bootstrap tags** — manually create tags for the current versions so release-please knows where history starts:

```bash
# Create tags at the current HEAD (or the commit just before phase 41)
git tag @cipherbox/api-v0.35.0 <sha>
git tag @cipherbox/web-v0.35.0 <sha>
git tag cipherbox-desktop-v0.35.0 <sha>
git tag cipherbox-tee-worker-v0.31.0 <sha>
# ... and for all other packages in the config
git push origin --tags
```

Option 2 is the more standard approach — once those tags exist, release-please will only generate changelogs from commits *after* those tags, so the next PR update will have a clean, concise changelog with only recent changes.

Want me to implement one of these?

> DEVELOPER

ok, so just creating the tags and rerunning the release please action should fix things up?

> AGENT

Yes, exactly. Release-please looks for existing tags to determine "last release." Once the tags exist, re-running the workflow will regenerate PR #427 with only commits since those tags — which should be just the phase 41 commits.

201	async function generateOpenApiSpec() {
202	  // Create app without starting HTTP server
203	  const app = await NestFactory.create(OpenApiGeneratorModule, {
204	    logger: false,
205	  });
206	
207	  const config = new DocumentBuilder()
208	    .setTitle('CipherBox API')
209	    .setDescription('Zero-knowledge encrypted cloud storage API')
210	    .setVersion('0.1.0')
211	    .addBearerAuth()
212	    .addTag('Health', 'Health check endpoints')
213	    .addTag('Root', 'API root endpoints')
214	    .addTag('Auth', 'Authentication endpoints')
215	    .addTag('Identity', 'CipherBox identity provider endpoints')
216	    .addTag('Vault', 'Vault management endpoints')
217	    .addTag('Files', 'File operations endpoints')
218	    .addTag('IPFS', 'IPFS relay endpoints')
219	    .addTag('IPNS', 'IPNS relay endpoints')
220	    .addTag('device-approval', 'Cross-device approval endpoints')
221	    .addTag('shares', 'User-to-user sharing endpoints')
222	    .addTag('invites', 'Public invite link endpoints')
223	    .addTag('share-invites', 'Authenticated invite management endpoints')
224	    .addTag('tee', 'TEE worker proxy endpoints')
225	    .build();
226	
227	  // Create document from the app
228	  const document = SwaggerModule.createDocument(app, config);
229	
230	  // Manually add health endpoint to spec (since we can't inject the real controller
231	  // without TypeORM dependencies)
232	  document.paths['/health'] = {
233	    get: {
234	      operationId: 'HealthController_check',
235	      summary: 'Check API and database health',
236	      tags: ['Health'],
237	      responses: {
238	        '200': {
239	          description: 'Health check passed',
240	          content: {
241	            'application/json': {
242	              schema: {
243	                type: 'object',
244	                properties: {
245	                  status: { type: 'string', example: 'ok' },
246	                  info: {
247	                    type: 'object',
248	                    properties: {
249	                      database: {
250	                        type: 'object',
251	                        properties: {
252	                          status: { type: 'string', example: 'up' },
253	                        },
254	                      },
255	                    },
256	                  },
257	                },
258	              },
259	            },
260	          },
261	        },
262	        '503': {
263	          description: 'Health check failed',
264	        },
265	      },
266	    },
267	  };
268	
269	  // Ensure output directory exists
270	  const outputDir = join(__dirname, '..', '..', '..', 'packages', 'api-client');
271	  mkdirSync(outputDir, { recursive: true });
272	
273	  const outputPath = join(outputDir, 'openapi.json');
274	  writeFileSync(outputPath, JSON.stringify(document, null, 2));
275	
276	  console.log(`OpenAPI spec written to: ${outputPath}`);
277	
278	  await app.close();
279	  process.exit(0);
280	}
281	
282	generateOpenApiSpec().catch((error) => {
283	  console.error('Failed to generate OpenAPI spec:', error);
284	  process.exit(1);
285	});

> AGENT

Actually, for a nested JSON field like `info.version`, release-please needs a jsonpath specification. A plain file path only works for `package.json`-style root `version` fields.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_015F7Fq1D1yMNtF1vDHY8aTM
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/release-please-config.json",
  "old_string": "      \"extra-files\": [\n        \"packages/api-client/openapi.json\"\n      ]",
  "new_string": "      \"extra-files\": [\n        {\n          \"type\": \"json\",\n          \"path\": \"packages/api-client/openapi.json\",\n          \"jsonpath\": \"$.info.version\"\n        }\n      ]"
}
```

Everything looks good. Here's a summary of the changes:

1. **Version alignment** — `@cipherbox/api-client` (`0.33.0` → `0.35.0`) and `cipherbox-api-client` (`0.4.0` → `0.35.0`) now match the API version
2. **OpenAPI version** — hardcoded `0.1.0` → `0.35.0` in `openapi.json`, and both `main.ts` and `generate-openapi.ts` now read from `npm_package_version` so it stays in sync
3. **Release-please config** — `openapi.json` added as an extra file with jsonpath so future releases bump it automatically
4. **Cargo.lock** — updated to reflect the crate version changes

1	# Phase 41: Package and App Versioning and Release Cycles - Discussion Log
2	
3	> **Audit trail only.** Do not use as input to planning, research, or execution agents.
4	> Decisions are captured in CONTEXT.md — this log preserves the alternatives considered.
5	
6	**Date:** 2026-03-31
7	**Phase:** 41-package-and-app-versioning-and-release-cycles
8	**Areas discussed:** Release signal source, Version grouping, Staging/deploy flow, CI enforcement
9	
10	---
11	
12	## Release Signal Source
13	
14	### Initial Direction: Changesets vs Release Please
15	
16	| Option                  | Description                                                  | Selected |
17	| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ | -------- |
18	| Changesets (replace RP) | Git-tracked changeset files survive squash, mature ecosystem |          |
19	| Release Please only     | Keep current setup, accept squash limitations                |          |
20	| Hybrid                  | Changesets for SDKs, RP for apps                             |          |
21	
22	**User's initial choice:** Changesets
23	
24	**Pivot moment:** User challenged the Changesets direction after Cargo.toml support gap surfaced. Key insight: "Release Please works, it's just the squash commits that are causing problems." This redirected the entire approach from replacing RP to enriching it.
25	
26	### Enriching RP with Intent Signals
27	
28	| Option              | Description                         | Selected    |
29	| ------------------- | ----------------------------------- | ----------- |
30	| PR labels           | Labels encode package + bump intent | ✓ (initial) |
31	| Custom intent files | .release-intent/ files per PR       |             |
32	| Full Changesets     | Accept ecosystem dependency         |             |
33	
34	**User's choice:** PR labels — zero files to manage, visible in PR UI.
35	
36	### Final Evolution: Auto-Analysis
37	
38	**Key insight from user:** "If we analyze the PR pre-merge, we can skip the manual label part entirely, as all the commits should follow conventional commitlint."
39	
40	| Option                                | Description                                                              | Selected |
41	| ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------- |
42	| Manual labels                         | Dev adds labels, CI validates                                            |          |
43	| Auto-analysis + labels for visibility | Action reads PR commits, auto-computes labels, manual override available | ✓        |
44	| Skip labels entirely                  | Compute bumps silently, no labels                                        |          |
45	
46	**User's choice:** Auto-analysis with labels for visibility and override capability.
47	
48	### RP Integration: release-as Injection
49	
50	| Option                  | Description                                      | Selected |
51	| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ | -------- |
52	| RP release-as injection | Post-merge action writes release-as to RP config | ✓        |
53	| Custom RP plugin        | TS plugin makes RP label-driven                  |          |
54	| Full Changesets         | Replace RP entirely                              |          |
55	| Accept imprecision      | Let RP path-match with squash commit type        |          |
56	
57	**User's choice:** release-as injection — precise, no plugin development, RP stays intact.
58	**User's note:** "I would really prefer to be precise about this as otherwise what is the point of all this work."
59

62	## Version Grouping
63	
64	### App Versioning
65	
66	| Option                           | Description                               | Selected |
67	| -------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- | -------- |
68	| Shared app version               | web + desktop + API all share one version |          |
69	| Independent app versions         | Each app versions separately              |          |
70	| Apps share, packages independent | Apps locked, packages free                |          |
71	
72	**User's choice:** All components version independently. User reconsidered the shared approach: "Initially I thought keeping web/api/desktop versions in sync made sense, but I am reconsidering."
73	
74	### API + Client Versioning
75	
76	| Option                    | Description                                 | Selected |
77	| ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- | -------- |
78	| Locked versions           | API + both clients always share version     | ✓        |
79	| API leads, clients follow | Separate versions with compatibility matrix |          |
80	| Spec-anchored             | OpenAPI spec version as shared contract     |          |
81	
82	**User's choice:** Locked versions. User noted that "all API consumers are consuming the API through the api-client based on the version in the openapi.json."
83	
84	**Detailed analysis provided:** Locked optimizes for simplicity (one number, zero confusion, phantom bumps). Spec-anchored optimizes for semantic accuracy (each version means something, but more complex mental model). User chose simplicity with note that migration to spec-anchored is straightforward later.
85	
86	### Rust Crate Versioning
87	
88	| Option                  | Description                                | Selected |
89	| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------ | -------- |
90	| Fully independent       | No relationship to TS counterpart versions | ✓        |
91	| Loosely coupled         | Major bumps mirror across languages        |          |
92	| Keep current divergence | Don't try to relate them                   |          |
93	
94	### Root Version
95	
96	| Option                   | Description                                    | Selected |
97	| ------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------- | -------- |
98	| Remove root version      | No monorepo-level version                      |          |
99	| Root = release milestone | Human-facing milestone marker, bumped manually | ✓        |
100	| Root tracks highest app  | Loosely coupled to highest app version         |          |
101	
102	### Component Versioning Strategies
103	
104	| Component     | Strategy                | Rationale                                       |
105	| ------------- | ----------------------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
106	| Web, Desktop  | Monotonic (major.minor) | Release counter, not semver                     |
107	| API + clients | Semver (locked)         | Contract-based, generated clients bound to spec |
108	| SDK packages  | Semver (independent)    | Library consumers need compatibility signals    |
109	| Rust crates   | Semver (independent)    | Separate implementations, different pace        |
110	| TEE worker    | Semver                  | Keeps options open, Docker tag matches          |
111	| Root          | Milestone               | Manual, significant releases only               |

> AGENT

The discussion log is clear at line 106:

> | Web, Desktop  | Monotonic (major.minor) | Release counter, not semver |

So you're right — the decision was monotonic versioning for web and desktop, not semver. But the implementation just used release-please's default `node` release type for all packages, which applies semver bumps everywhere.

To honor the original decision, we'd need to either:
1. Use a custom release-please strategy or `release-as` overrides to keep web/desktop on major.minor only
2. Or more practically — since release-please doesn't have a built-in "monotonic" mode — have the post-merge action force web/desktop bumps to always be minor (X.Y → X.Y+1) regardless of commit type, ignoring patch bumps entirely

Want me to look into how to implement this in the post-merge action?

> DEVELOPER

I guess semver is fine for now.

> AGENT

Fair enough — it's all internal anyway, and migrating later is straightforward if you ever want to. The discussion log has the decision recorded if you revisit it.

| 260401-5ft | Add API version to /health endpoint response | 1     | `apps/api/src/health/health.controller.ts`, `apps/api/src/health/health.controller.spec.ts` |

### Summary

Single task: modify the existing health controller to spread the version string (read from `apps/api/package.json`) into the Terminus `HealthCheckResult`, and add a unit test. The response changes from `{ status, info, error, details }` to `{ status, info, error, details, version }`. No api-client regeneration needed since the health endpoint is not consumed by the generated client.

{

  "content": "import { Test } from '@nestjs/testing';\nimport { HealthCheckService, TypeOrmHealthIndicator } from '@nestjs/terminus';\nimport { HealthController } from './health.controller';\n\ndescribe('HealthController', () => {\n  let controller: HealthController;\n\n  beforeEach(async () => {\n    const module = await Test.createTestingModule({\n      controllers: [HealthController],\n      providers: [\n        {\n          provide: HealthCheckService,\n          useValue: {\n            check: jest.fn().mockResolvedValue({\n              status: 'ok',\n              info: { database: { status: 'up' } },\n              error: {},\n              details: { database: { status: 'up' } },\n            }),\n          },\n        },\n        {\n          provide: TypeOrmHealthIndicator,\n          useValue: { pingCheck: jest.fn() },\n        },\n      ],\n    }).compile();\n\n    controller = module.get(HealthController);\n  });\n\n  it('should return version field matching semver pattern', async () => {\n    const result = await controller.check();\n    expect(result.version).toBeDefined();\n    expect(result.version).toMatch(/^\\d+\\.\\d+\\.\\d+/);\n  });\n\n  it('should preserve existing health check fields', async () => {\n    const result = await controller.check();\n    expect(result.status).toBe('ok');\n    expect(result.info).toEqual({ database: { status: 'up' } });\n    expect(result.details).toEqual({ database: { status: 'up' } });\n  });\n});\n"
}

> AGENT

It's just formatting churn — the JSON serialization changed from compact `["Root"]` to expanded multi-line arrays. No actual content change. This is because the generate script is now running with the version from `npm_package_version` which produces slightly different Swagger output formatting.

There's nothing meaningful to commit here — it's just whitespace reformatting of the same spec. I'd say skip it, or if you want a clean state, commit as `chore(api):` since it's just regenerated output with no functional change.

The GitHub PR commits API returns `commit.parents` as an array. Merge commits have 2+ parents. Simple fix:

> TOOL

id: toolu_01PAB7ev8C19grDTv2UJfRdy

The post-merge action is trying to push directly to `main`, but branch protection is blocking it — "12 of 12 required status checks are expected." The `github-actions[bot]` can't bypass branch protection.

Let me look at the workflow to understand what it's doing.

1	name: Post-Merge Release Targets
2	
3	on:
4	  push:
5	    branches:
6	      - main
7	
8	# Serialize concurrent runs to prevent race conditions writing to config (D-29 mitigation)
9	concurrency:
10	  group: post-merge-release
11	  cancel-in-progress: false
12	
13	permissions:
14	  contents: write
15	  pull-requests: read
16	
17	jobs:
18	  inject-release-targets:
19	    name: Inject Release Targets
20	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
21	    # Skip commits from release-please (its release PR merge) and our own release target commits
22	    if: >-
23	      !startsWith(github.event.head_commit.message, 'chore: release')
24	      && !startsWith(github.event.head_commit.message, 'chore(release): set release targets')
25	    steps:
26	      - uses: actions/create-github-app-token@v3
27	        id: app-token
28	        with:
29	          app-id: ${{ vars.RELEASE_BOT_APP_ID }}
30	          private-key: ${{ secrets.RELEASE_BOT_PRIVATE_KEY }}
31	
32	      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
33	        with:
34	          token: ${{ steps.app-token.outputs.token }}
35	          ref: main
36	
37	      - uses: actions/setup-node@v6
38	        with:
39	          node-version: '22'
40	
41	      - name: Install script dependencies
42	        run: npm install --no-save --no-package-lock --ignore-scripts @actions/core@1.11.1 @actions/github@6.0.0
43	
44	      - name: Compute and inject release targets
45	        id: inject
46	        env:
47	          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ steps.app-token.outputs.token }}
48	          COMMIT_SHA: ${{ github.sha }}
49	        run: node .github/scripts/post-merge-release.js
50	
51	      - name: Commit release targets
52	        if: steps.inject.outputs.packages_bumped != '[]' && steps.inject.outputs.packages_bumped != ''
53	        env:
54	          PR_NUMBER: ${{ steps.inject.outputs.pr_number }}
55	        run: |
56	          git config user.name "github-actions[bot]"
57	          git config user.email "41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com"
58	          git add release-please-config.json
59	          if git diff --staged --quiet; then
60	            echo "No changes to release-please-config.json — skipping commit"
61	            exit 0
62	          fi
63	          git commit -m "chore(release): set release targets from PR #${PR_NUMBER}"
64	          git push
65	
```

> AGENT

The issue is branch protection. The workflow uses a GitHub App token for the push (line 28-35), but the app isn't allowed to bypass required status checks on `main`.

You need to add the release bot GitHub App to the **"Allow specified actors to bypass required pull requests"** list in your branch protection rules:

**Settings > Branches > `main` rule > Allow specified actors to bypass required pull requests** — add the GitHub App (the one with `RELEASE_BOT_APP_ID`).

Alternatively, if you'd rather not grant bypass permissions, this workflow could be restructured to commit to the release-please PR branch instead of directly to `main`. But the simplest fix is the bypass rule.

9	 *   GITHUB_TOKEN        — GitHub App token (for API access)
10	 *   COMMIT_SHA          — The merge commit SHA on main
11	 *   GITHUB_REPOSITORY   — owner/repo
12	 *
13	 * Uses @actions/core, @actions/github, and fs (deps installed by the workflow).
14	 *
15	 * Design decisions:
16	 *   D-25: Find originating PR from commit SHA
17	 *   D-26: Compute target versions from labels
18	 *   D-27: Write release-as to RP config
19	 *   D-29: Handle existing release-as (take higher bump)
20	 *   D-30: RP consumes and clears release-as in release PR
21	 */
22	
23	import * as core from '@actions/core';
24	import * as github from '@actions/github';
25	import { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
26	import { join } from 'node:path';
27	import {
28	  BUMP_LEVELS,
29	  LABEL_TO_PATHS,
30	  LABEL_TYPE_TO_BUMP,
31	  MONOTONIC_PATHS,
32	} from './release-constants.js';
33	
34	// Alias for readability in this script
35	const BUMP_TYPE_TO_LEVEL = BUMP_LEVELS;
36	
37	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
38	// Helpers
39	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
40	
41	/**
42	 * Bump a semver version string by the given bump type.
43	 * For monotonic packages, patch is promoted to minor.
44	 *
45	 * @param {string} current — current version (e.g., "0.30.0" or "1.0")
46	 * @param {string} bumpType — "major", "minor", or "patch"
47	 * @param {boolean} [isMonotonic=false] — if true, patch -> minor
48	 * @returns {string} — the next version
49	 */
50	function bumpVersion(current, bumpType, isMonotonic = false) {
51	  const parts = current.split('.').map(Number);
52	  // Ensure 3-part semver (pad with 0 if needed)
53	  while (parts.length < 3) parts.push(0);
54	  const [major, minor, patch] = parts;
55	
56	  const effectiveBump = isMonotonic && bumpType === 'patch' ? 'minor' : bumpType;
57	
58	  switch (effectiveBump) {
59	    case 'major':
60	      return `${major + 1}.0.0`;
61	    case 'minor':
62	      return `${major}.${minor + 1}.0`;
63	    case 'patch':
64	      return `${major}.${minor}.${patch + 1}`;
65	    default:
66	      throw new Error(`Unknown bump type: ${effectiveBump}`);
67	  }
68	}
69	
70	/**
71	 * Compare two version strings to determine which represents a higher bump
72	 * from a base version. Returns true if `candidate` is a higher bump than
73	 * `existing`.
74	 *
75	 * @param {string} base — the current manifest version
76	 * @param {string} existing — existing release-as version
77	 * @param {string} candidate — newly computed release-as version
78	 * @returns {boolean} — true if candidate is a higher bump
79	 */
80	function isHigherBump(base, existing, candidate) {
81	  const baseParts = base.split('.').map(Number);
82	  while (baseParts.length < 3) baseParts.push(0);
83	
84	  const existingParts = existing.split('.').map(Number);
85	  while (existingParts.length < 3) existingParts.push(0);
86	
87	  const candidateParts = candidate.split('.').map(Number);
88	  while (candidateParts.length < 3) candidateParts.push(0);
89	
90	  // Compute delta from base for each
91	  const existingDelta = versionDelta(baseParts, existingParts);
92	  const candidateDelta = versionDelta(baseParts, candidateParts);
93	
94	  return candidateDelta > existingDelta;
95	}
96	
97	/**
98	 * Compare two version part arrays lexicographically (major, then minor, then patch).
99	 * Returns positive if `to` is higher than `from`, negative if lower, 0 if equal.
100	 *
101	 * @param {number[]} from
102	 * @param {number[]} to
103	 * @returns {number}
104	 */
105	function versionDelta(from, to) {
106	  return (to[0] - from[0]) * 10000 + (to[1] - from[1]) * 100 + (to[2] - from[2]);
107	}
108	
109	/**
110	 * Parse a release label into component and type.
111	 * Label format: release:{component}:{type}
112	 *
113	 * @param {string} label
114	 * @returns {{ component: string, type: string } | null}
115	 */
116	function parseReleaseLabel(label) {
117	  const parts = label.split(':');
118	  if (parts.length !== 3 || parts[0] !== 'release') return null;
119	  const component = parts[1];
120	  const type = parts[2];
121	  if (!LABEL_TO_PATHS[component]) return null;
122	  if (!LABEL_TYPE_TO_BUMP[type]) return null;
123	  return { component, type };
124	}
125	
126	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
127	// Main
128	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
129	
130	async function run() {
131	  const token = process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN;
132	  const commitSha = process.env.COMMIT_SHA;
133	  const repoFull = process.env.GITHUB_REPOSITORY;
134	
135	  if (!token || !commitSha || !repoFull) {
136	    core.setFailed(
137	      'Missing required environment variables: GITHUB_TOKEN, COMMIT_SHA, GITHUB_REPOSITORY'
138	    );
139	    return;
140	  }
141	
142	  const [owner, repo] = repoFull.split('/');
143	  const octokit = github.getOctokit(token);
144	
145	  // ------ Section 1: Find Originating PR (D-25) ------
146	  core.info(`Finding PR associated with commit ${commitSha}`);
147	
148	  const { data: prs } = await octokit.rest.repos.listPullRequestsAssociatedWithCommit({
149	    owner,
150	    repo,
151	    commit_sha: commitSha,
152	  });
153	
154	  const mergedPr = prs.find((pr) => pr.merged_at && pr.base.ref === 'main');
155	
156	  if (!mergedPr) {
157	    core.info('No merged PR found for this commit — nothing to do (direct push or non-main merge)');
158	    return;
159	  }
160	
161	  core.info(`Found merged PR #${mergedPr.number}: ${mergedPr.title}`);
162	
163	  // Skip Release Please's own release PRs
164	  if (mergedPr.head.ref.startsWith('release-please--')) {
165	    core.info('Skipping Release Please release PR — release-as injection not needed');
166	    return;
167	  }
168	
169	  // ------ Section 2: Read Labels ------
170	  const allLabels = mergedPr.labels.map((l) => l.name);
171	  core.info(`PR labels: ${allLabels.join(', ') || '(none)'}`);
172	
173	  // Check for release:none escape hatch
174	  if (allLabels.includes('release:none')) {
175	    core.info('release:none label present — skipping release-as injection');
176	    core.setOutput('packages_bumped', '[]');
177	    core.setOutput('pr_number', String(mergedPr.number));
178	    return;
179	  }
180	
181	  // Extract release labels
182	  const releaseLabels = allLabels
183	    .filter((l) => l.startsWith('release:') && l !== 'release:none')
184	    .map(parseReleaseLabel)
185	    .filter(Boolean);
186	
187	  if (releaseLabels.length === 0) {
188	    core.info('No release labels found on PR — nothing to inject');
189	    core.setOutput('packages_bumped', '[]');
190	    core.setOutput('pr_number', String(mergedPr.number));
191	    return;
192	  }
193	
194	  core.info(
195	    `Found ${releaseLabels.length} release label(s): ${releaseLabels.map((l) => `${l.component}:${l.type}`).join(', ')}`
196	  );
197	
198	  // ------ Section 3: Label-to-Path Mapping ------
199	  // Collect the highest bump per component from labels
200	  // (a single component could theoretically have multiple labels, take highest)
201	  /** @type {Map<string, string>} component -> bump type */
202	  const componentBumps = new Map();
203	
204	  for (const label of releaseLabels) {
205	    const bumpType = LABEL_TYPE_TO_BUMP[label.type];
206	    const existing = componentBumps.get(label.component);
207	    if (!existing || BUMP_TYPE_TO_LEVEL[bumpType] > BUMP_TYPE_TO_LEVEL[existing]) {
208	      componentBumps.set(label.component, bumpType);
209	    }
210	  }
211	
212	  // ------ Section 4 & 5: Read manifest, config, compute versions, handle existing release-as ------
213	  const configPath = join(process.cwd(), 'release-please-config.json');
214	  const manifestPath = join(process.cwd(), '.release-please-manifest.json');
215	
216	  const config = JSON.parse(readFileSync(configPath, 'utf8'));
217	  const manifest = JSON.parse(readFileSync(manifestPath, 'utf8'));
218	
219	  /** @type {Array<{path: string, component: string, currentVersion: string, targetVersion: string, bumpType: string}>} */
220	  const bumped = [];
221	
222	  for (const [component, bumpType] of componentBumps.entries()) {
223	    const paths = LABEL_TO_PATHS[component];
224	    if (!paths) {
225	      core.warning(`Unknown component "${component}" — skipping`);
226	      continue;
227	    }
228	
229	    for (const pkgPath of paths) {
230	      // Read current version from manifest
231	      const currentVersion = manifest[pkgPath];
232	      if (!currentVersion) {
233	        core.warning(`No version found in manifest for "${pkgPath}" — skipping`);
234	        continue;
235	      }
236	
237	      // Ensure package exists in config
238	      if (!config.packages[pkgPath]) {
239	        core.warning(`Package "${pkgPath}" not found in release-please-config.json — skipping`);
240	        continue;
241	      }
242	
243	      // Compute target version (D-26)
244	      const isMonotonic = MONOTONIC_PATHS.has(pkgPath);
245	      const targetVersion = bumpVersion(currentVersion, bumpType, isMonotonic);
246	
247	      // Handle existing release-as (D-29)
248	      const existingReleaseAs = config.packages[pkgPath]['release-as'];
249	      if (existingReleaseAs) {
250	        if (!isHigherBump(currentVersion, existingReleaseAs, targetVersion)) {
251	          core.info(
252	            `Keeping existing release-as ${existingReleaseAs} for ${pkgPath} (higher than computed ${targetVersion})`
253	          );
254	          continue;
255	        }
256	        core.info(
257	          `Overriding existing release-as ${existingReleaseAs} with ${targetVersion} for ${pkgPath} (higher bump)`
258	        );
259	      }
260	
261	      // Write release-as to config (D-27)
262	      config.packages[pkgPath]['release-as'] = targetVersion;
263	
264	      bumped.push({
265	        path: pkgPath,
266	        component,
267	        currentVersion,
268	        targetVersion,
269	        bumpType: isMonotonic && bumpType === 'patch' ? 'minor (monotonic)' : bumpType,
270	      });
271	    }
272	  }
273	
274	  if (bumped.length === 0) {
275	    core.info('No packages to bump — no changes to release-please-config.json');
276	    core.setOutput('packages_bumped', '[]');
277	    core.setOutput('pr_number', String(mergedPr.number));
278	    return;
279	  }
280	
281	  // ------ Section 6: Write updated config ------
282	  // Write updated config back to disk
283	  writeFileSync(configPath, JSON.stringify(config, null, 2) + '\n', 'utf8'); // release-please-config.json
284	  core.info(`Updated release-please-config.json with ${bumped.length} release-as entries`);
285	
286	  // ------ Section 7: Output Summary ------
287	  core.setOutput('packages_bumped', JSON.stringify(bumped));
288	  core.setOutput('pr_number', String(mergedPr.number));
289	
290	  // Write GitHub Actions step summary
291	  const summaryLines = [
292	    `## Release Targets from PR #${mergedPr.number}`,
293	    '',
294	    '| Package | Component | Current | Target | Bump |',
295	    '| ------- | --------- | ------- | ------ | ---- |',
296	  ];
297	
298	  for (const entry of bumped) {
299	    summaryLines.push(
300	      `| \`${entry.path}\` | ${entry.component} | ${entry.currentVersion} | ${entry.targetVersion} | ${entry.bumpType} |`
301	    );
302	  }
303	
304	  summaryLines.push('', `_release-as entries injected into release-please-config.json_`);
305	
306	  await core.summary.addRaw(summaryLines.join('\n')).write();
307	  core.info('Step summary written');
308	}
309	
310	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
311	// Entry point
312	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
313	
314	run().catch((err) => {
315	  core.setFailed(`Post-merge release target injection failed: ${err.message}`);
316	});
317	
```

> AGENT

OK so the release PR only has 3 packages (root, api, api-client) but the release preview labels had 7 more: `release:sdk-core:fix`, `release:sdk:fix`, `release:web:fix`, `release:cipherbox-fuse:fix`, `release:cipherbox-sdk:fix`, `release:tee-worker:fix`. Those were false positives from the merge commit (which we've now fixed with the merge commit filter).

So the release PR #430 actually looks correct — only root, api, and api-client were genuinely changed by PR #429. The extra labels were from the merge commit bringing in the version bump PR. The `fix(ci):` commit in PR #429 also touched `.github/scripts/` which doesn't map to any package.

The real issue is that the post-merge job couldn't push `release-as` targets to main. But since release-please is already computing the right bumps from the squash commit message `feat(api):`, the changelog is actually correct for this PR. The `release-as` injection is only needed when the squash commit message doesn't accurately represent all package bumps.

Now let me restructure the workflow to commit to the release-please branch instead.

> DEVELOPER

will committing directly to the release please branch actually work though? wont release please only pick up commits to `main`?

> AGENT

You're right — release-please reads `release-please-config.json` from `main` when it runs. Committing `release-as` to the release-please branch won't affect its behavior.

The options are:

1. **Grant the GitHub App bypass permissions on branch protection** — simplest, the current approach just works
2. **Create a PR for the config change** — post-merge action creates a tiny PR with the `release-as` injection, auto-merges it (but this adds noise and latency)
3. **Skip `release-as` entirely** — release-please already computes bumps from the squash commit message. The `release-as` injection is only needed when the squash message doesn't accurately represent package bumps (e.g., a `feat(api):` squash that also touched SDK files). How often will that actually happen?

Given that the release preview labels already validate the commit/label alignment pre-merge, and the squash commit type is what release-please uses, option 3 might be the pragmatic choice — you could remove the post-merge workflow entirely and only re-introduce it if you hit a case where release-please gets the bump wrong.

What's your preference?

> AGENT

Here's the play-by-play:

1. Go to **Settings > Branches** on the repo
2. Click **Edit** on the `main` branch protection rule
3. Scroll to **"Allow specified actors to bypass required pull requests"**
4. Search for and add your release bot GitHub App (the one configured with `RELEASE_BOT_APP_ID`)
5. Also check that the app is listed under **"Restrict who can push to matching branches"** if that section is enabled — it needs push access
6. Save changes

After that, re-run the failed workflow to verify:

```
gh run rerun 23829451708
```

The post-merge job will then be able to push `release-as` targets directly to `main` using the app token.

> DEVELOPER

So these are the options available to me. not seeing that `allow specified actors` option  [Image #1] [Image #2]

> AGENT

You're using the legacy branch protection rules — those don't have per-actor bypass. You have two paths:

**Option A: Switch to Repository Rulesets (recommended)**

The newer rulesets system has explicit bypass actor support:

1. Go to **Settings > Rules > Rulesets**
2. Create a new ruleset targeting `main`
3. Add the same protections you have now (require PR, require status checks, require linear history)
4. In the **Bypass list** at the top, add your release bot GitHub App
5. Delete the old legacy branch protection rule

**Option B: Quick fix with legacy rules**

Uncheck **"Do not allow bypassing the above settings"** — this lets actors with admin/bypass permissions push directly. The GitHub App would need the `administration` permission on the repo. Downside: human admins can also bypass.

Option A is cleaner since you can grant bypass to *only* the release bot. Want to go that route?

> DEVELOPER

I had some pretty strange issues with the rulesets previously.

> AGENT

Then go with Option B — uncheck **"Do not allow bypassing the above settings"**. That'll let the GitHub App token push directly to `main`.

The risk is minimal since you're the sole developer and approvals aren't required anyway. The only actors that could bypass are admins and the release bot app.

After unchecking, re-run the failed workflow:

```
gh run rerun 23829451708
```

502	
503	  for (const [component, computedLabel] of computedLabels.entries()) {
504	    const existing = existingByComponent.get(component);
505	    if (existing && existing !== computedLabel) {
506	      const existingLevel = LABEL_BUMP_LEVEL[existing.split(':')[2]] ?? 0;
507	      const computedLevel = LABEL_BUMP_LEVEL[computedLabel.split(':')[2]] ?? 0;
508	
509	      if (computedLevel > existingLevel) {
510	        // Computed bump is higher — replace existing label (e.g. fix→feat after force-push)
511	        labelsToRemove.push(existing);
512	        labelsToAdd.push(computedLabel);
513	      } else {
514	        // Existing label is higher or equal — treat as manual override
515	        manualOverrides.push({
516	          component,
517	          existing,
518	          computed: computedLabel,
519	        });
520	      }
521	    } else if (!existing) {
522	      labelsToAdd.push(computedLabel);
523	    }
524	    // If existing matches computed, no action needed
525	  }
526	
527	  // Remove labels for components no longer computed.
528	  // Manual overrides (D-18) still work but must be reapplied after the final commit.
529	  // TODO(deferred): preserve manually-added labels by tracking auto-applied state.
530	  for (const [component, existingLabel] of existingByComponent.entries()) {
531	    if (!computedLabels.has(component)) {
532	      labelsToRemove.push(existingLabel);
533	    }
534	  }
535	
536	  // Apply label changes
537	  for (const label of labelsToAdd) {
538	    try {
539	      await octokit.rest.issues.addLabels({
540	        owner,
541	        repo,
542	        issue_number: prNumber,
543	        labels: [label],
544	      });
545	      core.info(`Added label: ${label}`);
546	    } catch (err) {
547	      core.warning(`Could not add label "${label}": ${err.message}`);
548	    }
549	  }
550	
551	  for (const label of labelsToRemove) {
552	    try {
553	      await octokit.rest.issues.removeLabel({
554	        owner,
555	        repo,
556	        issue_number: prNumber,
557	        name: label,
558	      });
559	      core.info(`Removed label: ${label}`);
560	    } catch (err) {
561	      // Label might not exist — that's fine
562	      if (err.status !== 404) {
563	        core.warning(`Could not remove label "${label}": ${err.message}`);
564	      }
565	    }
566	  }
567	
568	  // ------ Section 8: PR Comment (D-23) ------
569	  const commentLines = [COMMENT_MARKER, '## Release Preview', ''];
570	
571	  if (componentBumps.size === 0 && warnings.length === 0) {
572	    commentLines.push(
573	      'No version bumps detected. All changes are in unversioned paths or use exempt commit types.'
574	    );
575	  } else {
576	    if (componentBumps.size > 0) {
577	      commentLines.push(
578	        '| Package | Bump | Label | Source |',
579	        '| ------- | ---- | ----- | ------ |'
580	      );
581	
582	      // Sort components alphabetically for stable output
583	      const sortedComponents = [...componentBumps.entries()].sort(([a], [b]) => a.localeCompare(b));
584	
585	      for (const [component, bumpInfo] of sortedComponents) {
586	        const label = computedLabels.get(component);
587	        const isDirect = !bumpInfo.source.startsWith('Cascade');
588	        const source = isDirect ? `Direct (${bumpInfo.label} commit)` : bumpInfo.source;
589	        commentLines.push(`| ${component} | ${bumpInfo.bump} | \`${label}\` | ${source} |`);
590	      }
591	    }
592	
593	    if (cascadeDetails.length > 0) {
594	      commentLines.push('', '### Cascade Details', '');
595	      // Deduplicate cascade details
596	      const seen = new Set();
597	      for (const detail of cascadeDetails) {
598	        const fromLabel = PATH_TO_LABEL[detail.from] || detail.from;
599	        const toLabel = PATH_TO_LABEL[detail.to] || detail.to;
600	        const key = `${fromLabel}->${toLabel}`;
601	        if (seen.has(key)) continue;
602	        seen.add(key);
603	        commentLines.push(
604	          `- \`${fromLabel}\` ${detail.fromBump} -> \`${toLabel}\` ${detail.toBump} (direct dependency)`
605	        );
606	      }
607	    }
608	
609	    if (manualOverrides.length > 0) {
610	      commentLines.push('', '### Manual Overrides', '');
611	      for (const override of manualOverrides) {
612	        commentLines.push(
613	          `- **${override.component}**: keeping manual label \`${override.existing}\` (computed: \`${override.computed}\`)`
614	        );
615	      }
616	    }
617	
618	    if (warnings.length > 0) {
619	      commentLines.push('', '### Warnings', '');
620	      for (const warning of warnings) {
621	        commentLines.push(`- ${warning}`);
622	      }
623	    }
624	  }
625	
626	  await postOrUpdateComment(octokit, owner, repo, prNumber, commentLines.join('\n'));
627	
628	  // ------ Section 9: CI Check Status (D-19) ------
629	  if (warnings.length > 0) {
630	    core.setFailed(
631	      `${warnings.length} commit(s) touching versioned packages have non-conventional messages. ` +
632	        'Fix commit messages or add the `release:none` label to skip enforcement.'
633	    );
634	  }
635	}
636	
637	/**
638	 * Post or update the release preview comment on the PR.
639	 * @param {ReturnType<typeof github.getOctokit>} octokit
640	 * @param {string} owner
641	 * @param {string} repo
642	 * @param {number} prNumber
643	 * @param {string} body
644	 */
645	async function postOrUpdateComment(octokit, owner, repo, prNumber, body) {
646	  // Find existing comment with marker
647	  const comments = await fetchAllPages(
648	    octokit,
649	    'GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/{issue_number}/comments',
650	    { owner, repo, issue_number: prNumber }
651	  );
652	
653	  const existing = comments.find(
654	    (c) => c.body && c.body.includes(COMMENT_MARKER) && c.user?.login === 'github-actions[bot]'
655	  );
656	
657	  if (existing) {
658	    await octokit.rest.issues.updateComment({
659	      owner,
660	      repo,
661	      comment_id: existing.id,
662	      body,
663	    });
664	    core.info(`Updated release preview comment (ID: ${existing.id})`);
665	  } else {
666	    await octokit.rest.issues.createComment({
667	      owner,
668	      repo,
669	      issue_number: prNumber,
670	      body,
671	    });
672	    core.info('Created release preview comment');
673	  }
674	}
675	
676	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
677	// Entry point
678	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
679	
680	run().catch((err) => {
681	  core.setFailed(`PR release preview failed: ${err.message}`);
682	});
683	
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/.github/scripts/pr-release-preview.js",
  "old_string": "  // ------ Section 8: PR Comment (D-23) ------",
  "new_string": "  // ------ Section 7.5: Release-As Injection ------\n  // Write release-as targets into release-please-config.json on the PR branch.\n  // When the PR merges, these land on main and release-please picks them up.\n  const configPath = join(process.cwd(), 'release-please-config.json');\n  const manifestPath = join(process.cwd(), '.release-please-manifest.json');\n  const config = JSON.parse(readFileSync(configPath, 'utf8'));\n  const manifest = JSON.parse(readFileSync(manifestPath, 'utf8'));\n\n  /** @type {Array<{path: string, currentVersion: string, targetVersion: string, bumpType: string}>} */\n  const releaseAsEntries = [];\n  let configChanged = false;\n\n  // First, clear any stale release-as entries for packages NOT being bumped in this PR\n  for (const [pkgPath, pkgConfig] of Object.entries(config.packages)) {\n    if (pkgConfig['release-as'] && !packageBumps.has(pkgPath)) {\n      delete pkgConfig['release-as'];\n      configChanged = true;\n      core.info(`Cleared stale release-as for ${pkgPath}`);\n    }\n  }\n\n  for (const [pkgPath, bumpInfo] of packageBumps.entries()) {\n    const currentVersion = manifest[pkgPath];\n    if (!currentVersion || !config.packages[pkgPath]) continue;\n\n    const isMonotonic = MONOTONIC_PATHS.has(pkgPath);\n    const bumpType = bumpInfo.bump;\n    const effectiveBump = isMonotonic && bumpType === 'patch' ? 'minor' : bumpType;\n\n    const parts = currentVersion.split('.').map(Number);\n    while (parts.length < 3) parts.push(0);\n    const [major, minor, patch] = parts;\n\n    let targetVersion;\n    switch (effectiveBump) {\n      case 'major': targetVersion = `${major + 1}.0.0`; break;\n      case 'minor': targetVersion = `${major}.${minor + 1}.0`; break;\n      case 'patch': targetVersion = `${major}.${minor}.${patch + 1}`; break;\n      default: continue;\n    }\n\n    const existingReleaseAs = config.packages[pkgPath]['release-as'];\n    if (existingReleaseAs !== targetVersion) {\n      config.packages[pkgPath]['release-as'] = targetVersion;\n      configChanged = true;\n    }\n\n    releaseAsEntries.push({ path: pkgPath, currentVersion, targetVersion, bumpType: effectiveBump });\n  }\n\n  if (configChanged) {\n    writeFileSync(configPath, JSON.stringify(config, null, 2) + '\\n', 'utf8');\n    core.info(`Updated release-please-config.json with ${releaseAsEntries.length} release-as entries`);\n  } else {\n    core.info('release-please-config.json unchanged — no commit needed');\n  }\n  core.setOutput('config_changed', String(configChanged));\n\n  // ------ Section 8: PR Comment (D-23) ------"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/.github/workflows/pr-release-preview.yml",
  "content": "name: PR Release Preview\n\non:\n  pull_request:\n    branches: [main]\n    types: [opened, synchronize, reopened, labeled, unlabeled]\n\npermissions:\n  contents: write\n  pull-requests: write\n\njobs:\n  release-preview:\n    name: Release Preview\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    # Skip release-please's own PRs and our own release-as commits\n    if: >-\n      !startsWith(github.head_ref, 'release-please--')\n      && github.event.pull_request.user.login != 'github-actions[bot]'\n    steps:\n      - uses: actions/checkout@v6\n        with:\n          ref: ${{ github.head_ref }}\n          fetch-depth: 0\n\n      - uses: actions/setup-node@v6\n        with:\n          node-version: '22'\n\n      - name: Install script dependencies\n        run: npm install --no-save --no-package-lock --ignore-scripts @actions/core@1.11.1 @actions/github@6.0.0\n\n      - name: Analyze PR commits and apply release labels\n        id: preview\n        env:\n          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}\n          PR_NUMBER: ${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}\n        run: node .github/scripts/pr-release-preview.js\n\n      - name: Commit release-as targets\n        if: steps.preview.outputs.config_changed == 'true'\n        env:\n          PR_NUMBER: ${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}\n        run: |\n          git config user.name \"github-actions[bot]\"\n          git config user.email \"41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com\"\n          git add release-please-config.json\n          if git diff --staged --quiet; then\n            echo \"No changes to commit\"\n            exit 0\n          fi\n          git commit -m \"chore(release): set release targets for PR #${PR_NUMBER}\"\n          git push\n"
}

{
  "command": "gh pr create --title \"fix(ci): move release-as injection from post-merge to PR branch\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n- Moves release-as target computation from the post-merge workflow (which couldn't push to `main` due to branch protection) into the PR release preview workflow\n- The preview workflow now writes `release-as` entries to `release-please-config.json` on the feature branch and commits them\n- When the PR merges, the targets land on `main` naturally and release-please picks them up\n- Removes the now-unnecessary `post-merge-release.yml` workflow and `post-merge-release.js` script\n\n## How it works\n1. PR opened/updated → release preview runs, computes labels + release-as targets\n2. If `release-please-config.json` changed, commits to the PR branch\n3. PR merges → config with `release-as` entries lands on main\n4. Release-please processes entries and clears them in the release PR\n\nPushes via `GITHUB_TOKEN` don't trigger new workflow runs (GitHub's built-in recursion prevention), so no infinite loop.\n\n## Test plan\n- [ ] Release preview workflow runs and commits `release-as` to this PR's branch\n- [ ] No infinite loop from the commit triggering another run\n- [ ] After merge, release-please picks up the `release-as` targets\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Create PR"
}
```

          "body": "Release-as target computation is based on `packageBumps` (commit-derived), but the workflow explicitly supports manual label overrides (D-18) where existing PR labels can be higher than computed. With the current logic, a manual override label can be applied to the PR while the injected `release-as` still reflects the lower commit-derived bump, so release-please will cut the wrong version. Compute `release-as` from the final effective labels (including manual overrides), e.g., by mapping label types via `LABEL_TYPE_TO_BUMP`/`LABEL_TO_PATHS`.\n```suggestion\n  /**\n   * Compare two semver-like version strings (e.g. \"1.2.3\").\n   * Returns 1 if a > b, -1 if a < b, 0 if equal or unparsable.\n   */\n  function compareSemver(a, b) {\n    if (!a || !b) return 0;\n    const aParts = String(a).split('.').map((n) => Number.parseInt(n, 10));\n    const bParts = String(b).split('.').map((n) => Number.parseInt(n, 10));\n    while (aParts.length < 3) aParts.push(0);\n    while (bParts.length < 3) bParts.push(0);\n    for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) {\n      const av = Number.isNaN(aParts[i]) ? 0 : aParts[i];\n      const bv = Number.isNaN(bParts[i]) ? 0 : bParts[i];\n      if (av > bv) return 1;\n      if (av < bv) return -1;\n    }\n    return 0;\n  }\n\n  for (const [pkgPath, bumpInfo] of packageBumps.entries()) {\n    const currentVersion = manifest[pkgPath];\n    if (!currentVersion || !config.packages[pkgPath]) continue;\n\n    const isMonotonic = MONOTONIC_PATHS.has(pkgPath);\n    const bumpTypeFromCommits = bumpInfo.bump;\n    const effectiveBumpFromCommits =\n      isMonotonic && bumpTypeFromCommits === 'patch' ? 'minor' : bumpTypeFromCommits;\n\n    const parts = currentVersion.split('.').map(Number);\n    while (parts.length < 3) parts.push(0);\n    const [major, minor, patch] = parts;\n\n    let targetVersionFromCommits;\n    switch (effectiveBumpFromCommits) {\n      case 'major':\n        targetVersionFromCommits = `${major + 1}.0.0`;\n        break;\n      case 'minor':\n        targetVersionFromCommits = `${major}.${minor + 1}.0`;\n        break;\n      case 'patch':\n        targetVersionFromCommits = `${major}.${minor}.${patch + 1}`;\n        break;\n      default:\n        continue;\n    }\n\n    const existingReleaseAs = config.packages[pkgPath]['release-as'];\n\n    // Never downgrade an existing higher release-as version (e.g. from manual overrides).\n    let finalReleaseAsVersion = targetVersionFromCommits;\n    if (existingReleaseAs) {\n      const cmp = compareSemver(existingReleaseAs, targetVersionFromCommits);\n      if (cmp > 0) {\n        finalReleaseAsVersion = existingReleaseAs;\n      }\n    }\n\n    if (existingReleaseAs !== finalReleaseAsVersion) {\n      config.packages[pkgPath]['release-as'] = finalReleaseAsVersion;\n      configChanged = true;\n    }\n\n    // Derive the effective bump type from currentVersion -> finalReleaseAsVersion\n    const finalParts = String(finalReleaseAsVersion)\n      .split('.')\n      .map((n) => Number.parseInt(n, 10));\n    while (finalParts.length < 3) finalParts.push(0);\n    const [tMajor, tMinor, tPatch] = finalParts;\n\n    let effectiveBump;\n    if (tMajor > major) {\n      effectiveBump = 'major';\n    } else if (tMinor > minor) {\n      effectiveBump = 'minor';\n    } else if (tPatch > patch) {\n      effectiveBump = 'patch';\n    } else {\n      // No actual version increase; skip entry.\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    releaseAsEntries.push({\n      path: pkgPath,\n      currentVersion,\n      targetVersion: finalReleaseAsVersion,\n```",

{

  "new_string": "  if (existingLabels.includes('release:none')) {\n    core.info('release:none label present — skipping enforcement and label computation');\n\n    // Clear any previously injected release-as entries\n    const configPath = join(process.cwd(), 'release-please-config.json');\n    const config = JSON.parse(readFileSync(configPath, 'utf8'));\n    let cleaned = false;\n    for (const pkgConfig of Object.values(config.packages)) {\n      if (pkgConfig['release-as']) {\n        delete pkgConfig['release-as'];\n        cleaned = true;\n      }\n    }\n    if (cleaned) {\n      writeFileSync(configPath, JSON.stringify(config, null, 2) + '\\n', 'utf8');\n      core.info('Cleared stale release-as entries from release-please-config.json');\n      core.setOutput('config_changed', 'true');\n    } else {\n      core.setOutput('config_changed', 'false');\n    }\n\n    // Post a minimal comment\n    await postOrUpdateComment(\n      octokit,\n      owner,\n      repo,\n      prNumber,\n      [\n        COMMENT_MARKER,\n        '## Release Preview',\n        '',\n        '`release:none` label present — no version bumps for this PR.',\n      ].join('\\n')\n    );\n    return;\n  }"

{
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/.github/workflows/pr-release-preview.yml",
  "content": "name: PR Release Preview\n\non:\n  pull_request:\n    branches: [main]\n    types: [opened, synchronize, reopened, labeled, unlabeled]\n\nconcurrency:\n  group: release-preview-${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}\n  cancel-in-progress: true\n\npermissions:\n  contents: write\n  pull-requests: write\n\njobs:\n  release-preview:\n    name: Release Preview\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    # Skip release-please's own PRs — they don't need release labels\n    if: \"!startsWith(github.head_ref, 'release-please--')\"\n    steps:\n      - uses: actions/checkout@v6\n        with:\n          ref: ${{ github.head_ref }}\n          fetch-depth: 0\n\n      - uses: actions/setup-node@v6\n        with:\n          node-version: '22'\n\n      - name: Install script dependencies\n        run: npm install --no-save --no-package-lock --ignore-scripts @actions/core@1.11.1 @actions/github@6.0.0\n\n      - name: Analyze PR commits and apply release labels\n        id: preview\n        env:\n          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}\n          PR_NUMBER: ${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}\n        run: node .github/scripts/pr-release-preview.js\n\n      - name: Commit release-as targets\n        if: >-\n          steps.preview.outputs.config_changed == 'true'\n          && github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name == github.repository\n        env:\n          PR_NUMBER: ${{ github.event.pull_request.number }}\n        run: |\n          git config user.name \"github-actions[bot]\"\n          git config user.email \"41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com\"\n          git add release-please-config.json\n          if git diff --staged --quiet; then\n            echo \"No changes to commit\"\n            exit 0\n          fi\n          git commit -m \"chore(release): set release targets for PR #${PR_NUMBER}\"\n          git push\n"
}
```

existing higher-or-equal release label is treated as a manual override in the
else branch (inside the loop over computedLabels where
existingByComponent.get(component) exists), persist that override into the
package-level bump map so later release-as derivation uses it: after pushing the
manualOverrides entry (and before continuing), update the packageBumps (or
computedLabels) entry for that component to reflect the existing label (e.g.,
set packageBumps[component] / computedLabels.set(component, existing)) so the
reconciled label is fed back into the release-as generation; apply the same
change to the analogous block handling the other pass (the section referenced
for lines 589-627).

> AGENT

This is a real issue. Here's the flow:

1. Commits compute `packageBumps` with e.g. `apps/api` → `patch` (from a `fix` commit)
2. But someone manually set label `release:api:feat` on the PR
3. Section 7 correctly preserves that manual label (lines 532-538)
4. But `packageBumps` still has `patch` — Section 7.5 derives `release-as` from `packageBumps`, so it writes a patch bump instead of the minor the manual label intended

The fix: after detecting a manual override, update `packageBumps` for the affected packages to reflect the higher label bump.

> TOOL

_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**Preserve inherited `release-as` entries instead of rebuilding from this PR alone.**

In `release-please-config.json` (lines 18-117), none of the packages have a static `release-as`, so any value already present on the checked-out branch is pending release state, not noise. Line 582 deletes unrelated pending targets outright, and Lines 616-618 can also downgrade an existing higher target because the recompute is anchored to `.release-please-manifest.json` rather than the branch’s current `release-as`. A later PR can therefore erase or lower a target that an earlier PR already queued but release-please has not cleared yet.

Please use the base-branch config as the baseline (or otherwise track which entries this PR introduced) before clearing or lowering anything.



Also applies to: 589-620

🌐 Web query:

`release-please bump-minor-pre-major configuration pre-1.0 breaking changes`

💡 Result:

The "bump-minor-pre-major" configuration option in release-please changes how semantic versioning bumps are calculated for packages with versions less than 1.0.0 (pre-major versions). When enabled (set to true), it treats breaking changes (indicated by Conventional Commits with ! or BREAKING CHANGE footers) as minor version bumps instead of major bumps. This follows SemVer conventions where pre-1.0.0 versions use major for incompatible changes within the 0.y.z range, but allows more conservative bumping to avoid jumping to 1.0.0 prematurely. It can be configured globally or per-package in release-please-config.json (manifest mode): { "$schema": "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/googleapis/release-please/main/schemas/config.json", "packages": { ".": { "release-type": "node", "bump-minor-pre-major": true } } } A related option "bump-patch-for-minor-pre-major" (when true) makes feat commits bump patch instead of minor pre-1.0.0. For initial releases (no prior tag), the first version is determined by release strategy or "Release-As" commit, not bumping logic. There is no specific handling for breaking changes pre-1.0.0 beyond this config; it applies to ongoing bumps after initial release. No breaking changes related to this option appear in recent changelogs up to v17.3.0 (2026-02-18).

Citations:

- 1: https://github.com/googleapis/release-please/issues/1209
- 2: https://github.com/googleapis/release-please/blob/main/docs/customizing.md
- 3: https://elixirschool.com/blog/managing-releases-with-release-please
- 4: https://github.com/googleapis/release-please/blob/refs/heads/main/docs/manifest-releaser.md
- 5: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/googleapis/release-please/main/schemas/config.json

---



</details>

**Apply `bump-minor-pre-major` logic when computing target versions.**

All packages in `.release-please-manifest.json` are on `0.x` versions, and `.release-please-config.json` sets `bump-minor-pre-major: true` for each package. This instructs release-please to treat breaking changes as minor bumps (not major) while pre-1.0.0. Lines 603–605 currently force `1.0.0` for any breaking bump, bypassing this config. The first breaking PR would inject an unintended major release.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
+    const bumpMinorPreMajor = config.packages[pkgPath]['bump-minor-pre-major'] === true;
+    const resolvedBump =
+      major === 0 &&
+      effectiveBump === 'major' &&
+      bumpMinorPreMajor
+        ? 'minor'
+        : effectiveBump;
+
     let targetVersion;
-    switch (effectiveBump) {
+    switch (resolvedBump) {
       case 'major':
         targetVersion = `${major + 1}.0.0`;
         break;
```
</details>

effectiveBump and currentVersion) to consult the package's release-please config
flag (bump-minor-pre-major) and, when currentVersion's major === 0 and
bump-minor-pre-major is true, treat a 'major' bump as a 'minor' (i.e., set
effectiveBump = 'minor' instead of allowing the switch case that makes
`${major+1}.0.0`); ensure the switch that sets targetVersion for 'major' remains
for true majors but is not used for pre-1.0 packages with bump-minor-pre-major

> AGENT

The logs just show container cleanup — the actual failure is further up. But the root cause doesn't matter for unblocking the release PR. The issue is:

1. PR #431's CI E2E skipped Web E2E (no web changes)
2. The "Verify E2E Passed" check falls back to the last run where Web E2E ran, which happened to fail

The quickest fix to unblock: re-run the CI E2E workflow on main with Web E2E forced, or re-run the failed check on the release PR.

> AGENT

The release PR (#430) regenerates the OpenAPI spec and the committed version differs from what the generator produces. This is the release PR — release-please bumps version numbers in `package.json` files, which changes `npm_package_version`, which changes the version in the generated OpenAPI spec.

The release PR has `@cipherbox/api` at `0.36.0`, but the committed `openapi.json` still says `0.35.0`. The generator reads the bumped `package.json` version and produces `0.36.0`, which doesn't match what's committed.

This is expected — release-please bumps the `package.json` but doesn't know to regenerate the OpenAPI spec. The `extra-files` jsonpath config we added should handle this. Let me check if release-please actually updated the version in `openapi.json` in the release PR.

> AGENT

No `openapi.json` change. The `extra-files` with jsonpath isn't being picked up by release-please. The issue is that `openapi.json` is listed under `apps/api`'s extra-files, but the file is at `packages/api-client/openapi.json` — release-please resolves extra-file paths relative to the package path (`apps/api/`), so it's looking for `apps/api/packages/api-client/openapi.json`.

> AGENT

Yep — the path `packages/api-client/openapi.json` is relative to `apps/api/`, so release-please looks for `apps/api/packages/api-client/openapi.json` which doesn't exist. Need to use `../../packages/api-client/openapi.json` or move this to the root package config.

The cleanest fix: move it to the root package (`.`) since it's a cross-package file, or use a relative path that escapes `apps/api/`.

> AGENT

PR: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/432

The `Verify API Spec & Client` failure on PR #430 is because release-please bumped `package.json` to `0.36.0` but never touched `openapi.json` (still `0.35.0`). The extra-files path was relative to `apps/api/` so `packages/api-client/openapi.json` resolved to a nonexistent `apps/api/packages/api-client/openapi.json`. Once #432 merges, release-please should regenerate PR #430 with the correct `openapi.json` version bump.

```
Excluding only `info.version` from the semantic comparison won’t fully address version-driven diffs on release PRs: `packages/api-client/openapi.json` also embeds the API package version in the `/health` schema (`paths['/health'].get.responses['200']...properties.version.example`). That field will still differ after release-please bumps `apps/api`’s version, so this check can still fail even when the schema itself hasn’t changed. Consider also stripping that `/health` version example (and any other version-derived fields) before comparing, or stop embedding the version as an OpenAPI `example` value at generation time.
```suggestion
            // Normalize out version-derived fields that change on release bumps
            function sanitize(doc) {
              if (!doc || typeof doc !== 'object') return;
              // Exclude top-level info.version — it's dynamic from package.json and differs on release PRs
              if (doc.info && Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(doc.info, 'version')) {
                delete doc.info.version;
              }
              // Exclude version example in /health response schema, which also embeds the package version
              const healthVersion =
                doc.paths &&
                doc.paths['/health'] &&
                doc.paths['/health'].get &&
                doc.paths['/health'].get.responses &&
                doc.paths['/health'].get.responses['200'] &&
                doc.paths['/health'].get.responses['200'].content &&
                doc.paths['/health'].get.responses['200'].content['application/json'] &&
                doc.paths['/health'].get.responses['200'].content['application/json'].schema &&
                doc.paths['/health'].get.responses['200'].content['application/json'].schema.properties &&
                doc.paths['/health'].get.responses['200'].content['application/json'].schema.properties.version;
              if (healthVersion && Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(healthVersion, 'example')) {
                delete healthVersion.example;
              }
            }
            sanitize(committed);
            sanitize(generated);
```
```
